### Page 2

世界経済：AI・防衛関連需要を受け景況感は好調。一方でインフレ圧力の広がりも確認
 9月のグローバル製造業PMIは53.0（8月52.3）と堅調。非製造業は54.5（8月53.6）と40か月ぶりの高水準
― 製造業はAI・防衛関連投資需要を受け生産、新規受注ともに上昇。非製造業も幅広い業種で業況が改善
― 一方、仕入・販売価格指数はともに上昇。エネルギーや輸送価格上昇によるインフレ圧力の広がりを示唆
 国・地域別では米国・ユーロ圏が製造業・非製造業ともに改善。とりわけ米国の好調が目立つ
― 米国ではAI関連支出の拡大に伴い機械・設備投資および生産が増加。非製造業においても情報通信業が突出して好調
― ユーロ圏でも、AI投資に支えられたIT関連サービスが特に力強く拡大。専門・商業サービスも改善
1
製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数） 非製造業の景況感（購買担当者景気指数）
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数：企業に対するアン
ケート調査結果から作成した指数。新規受注や生産高、雇用、出荷遅延などの指数

に一定のウエイトを掛けて算出。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点
（出所）S&P Global、中国国家統計局より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
（注） 日米欧：S&P Global、中：国家統計局。購買担当者景気指数（非製造業）：企業に対
するアンケート調査結果から作成した指数。50が景気拡大・縮小の分岐点

### Page 3

金融市場概況：投資家心理が悪化し、日米とも株安・金利低下
 AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、日米株価はハイテク株を中心に下落
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機に。原油高に伴うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に
― 投資家心理が悪化するなかで、安全資産とされる日米国債に資金が流入、前月までの金利上昇基調は一巡
 ドル円は、1ドル＝150円台後半で方向感の乏しい展開
― 日米金利とも低下し、日米金利差は概ね横ばい圏で推移
― 原油高が輸入増を通じて円安要因となる一方、日米株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化に伴うユーロ安は円高要因に
2

米国：インフレ率は高止まり、油価再加速で当面は物価高が続く懸念
 原油高や関税の影響から物価は加速
―9月のコアCPIは前月比＋0.3％（年率＋4.1％）と8月（前月比＋0.3％、年率＋3.5％）から伸びが拡大
― コアサービスでは住居費や輸送サービスなどが押し上げに寄与。コア財については、衣料品や玩具などが上昇。関税引
き上げに伴う価格転嫁が続いている模様
 足元で原油価格が再加速していることを踏まえれば、当面は物価の上振れリスクが残存する懸念
― シカゴ連銀が公表する週次の小売物価指標は9月後半から10月初めにかけて上昇しており、物価の高止まりを示唆
3
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
項目別コアCPI 週次小売物価（シカゴ連銀）

（出所） シカゴ連銀より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：足元の消費はガソリン高や関税に伴う物価上昇で足踏みも、年末商戦は堅調な見通し
 消費は足踏み。ガソリン価格高騰や関税に伴う物価高が家計の購買力を下押し
―9月の実質小売売上高は前月比▲0.5％と減少。ガソリン小売が減少したほか、自動車や衣料品も低調に推移
 物価高は逆風も、2026年の年末商戦は堅調予想
― 米マスターカードは、実質小売売上高（除くガソリン・自動車）は前年比＋2.7％と、前年（同＋2.1％）から伸びが拡大する
と予想。雇用環境の改善などを背景に幅広い所得層で安定した消費がみられると指摘
4
（注） ガソリンはCPIを用いて実質化。その他は、8、9月のみ各品目に対応するCPIを用

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同＋8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建築・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

5
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 7

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様
6
（注）1.バブルの大きさは各産業の雇用者数
2. 対人接触度は、O*NETの職業別のPhysical Proximity（業務上、他者と物理的に近
接する度合い）を、各産業の職業別雇用者構成で加重平均した指数
3.コロナ禍からの雇用回復の影響を除くため、雇用者数の変化率は2023年末を起点
に算出
（出所） 米労働省、セントルイス連銀より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：生産は底堅く推移。AI・防衛が投資財生産をけん引、半導体増産も寄与
 2026年8月の鉱工業生産は前年比＋1.4％と底堅い結果
― 半導体関連の生産が引き続きけん引。生産能力が2022年対比1.7倍に拡大するなど生産体制の増強が背景。事実、主
要半導体メーカーによる工場新設・増強を受け、MOSロジック（GPU、CPUなど演算用）の量産が本格化
 AI・防衛分野を中心に投資財生産も堅調さを維持
― 投資関連財はAI・防衛が押し上げる形で同＋4.7％と増勢継続。国防・宇宙関連設備は同＋6.1％ と全体を上回る伸び。
地政学リスクの高まりを背景に軍用機やミサイル・防空システムなどの生産体制強化が図られている模様
7

生活費
期待より悪い 期待通り 期待より良い
（シェア）
米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

8

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
9
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
EUの石油製品消費はディーゼル・軽油が中心

### Page 11

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
10
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（出所）Eurostat より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：EU・中国はハイブリッド車の輸入制限で暫定合意。同様の措置は他の品目に広がる可能性
11
 EUと中国は、貿易不均衡の是正措置について暫定合意。中国からのハイブリッド車輸入は従来見通しの半分以下に
―EUが中国産EVに追加関税を賦課した2024年以降、追加関税の対象とならないハイブリッド車輸入が急増
― 欧州委員会の試算では、今回の措置によって2030年までの4年間で数百万台の輸入を削減見込み。直近では、EUが輸
入するハイブリッド車の約半分を中国車が占めており、域内自動車市場に一定のインパクト
 欧州委員会は、幅広い産業で貿易不均衡を是正する必要性を示唆。同様の措置が他の品目にも広がる可能性
― 欧州委員会のモニタリング対象（輸入増加と価格下落が併存する品目）は、中国からの輸入品が最多

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
12
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を免除する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超過。

― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
13
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性

中国：7～9月期の実質GDPは＋4.5％。インフラ投資の加速で、通年の成長率目標を確保へ
 7～9月期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.5％。1～9月の累計で同＋4.6％と、通年目標「＋4.5～5.0％」の範囲内
―7～9月期は、純輸出が＋1.8％Ptと成長をけん引する一方、総資本形成は＋0.6％Ptまで縮小。内需の弱さ鮮明
― 中国政府は地方政府専項債の発行枠を追加（2,500億元）。インフラ投資を加速させ、成長率目標の達成を確保へ
 9月の小売は同＋1.1％と、前月（同＋0.4％）から加速。メモリー高騰が通信機器の売上額（同＋31.1％）を押し上げ
― 投資は同▲7.2％（前月：同▲10.6％） 。前年の反動増は限定的で、インフラ投資は同▲10.5％と2ケタ減が続く

― 生産は同＋5.5％（前月：同＋5.2％）。生産量では、産業用ロボット（同＋18.9％）や集積回路（同＋18.4％）が堅調

中国：政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ。人民元相場は緩やかな元高が継続
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え
 人民元相場は、中国の貿易黒字が過去最高水準を記録する中、緩やかに元高が進行し1ドル＝6.6元台に突入
― 高水準の貿易黒字を背景とした人民元買いの実需が強いことから、当面は緩やかな元高が続く見込み
― ただ、米FRBの利上げで米中金利差が拡大しており、さらなる拡大が意識されれば元安に転じる可能性も

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
16
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

― 積み上がった受注残を段階的に消化することで、設備投資が持続的に下支えされる公算が大きい

日本：コメ価格下落で企業物価は鈍化。もっとも、先行きは原油高を背景に再加速へ
18
 9月の国内企業物価はコメ価格の下落を主因に、前年比＋6.8％（前月：同＋7.6％）と鈍化
― 新米価格が昨年を下回ったこと等を背景に、コメ価格が大幅に下落。国内企業物価を▲0.75%Pt押し下げ
― 一方、3月来の原油高を背景に化学・プラスチック製品は高止まり。また、AI需要等を背景に非鉄金属も高い伸びを維持
 7月以降の原油高再燃を背景に、先行きの国内企業物価は再加速する見込み
― 原油価格高止まりにタンカー運賃の急騰が重なり、先行きの輸入物価は原油関連を中心に加速へ
― 国内企業物価は石油・石炭製品やラグを伴いつつ化学・プラスチック製品などを中心に再加速する見込み
（注） コメは玄米及び精米。食品関連は、飲食料品及び農林水産物（除くコメ）

米国債券市場：インフレ加速懸念で上昇後、AI関連株の下落を受け金利は低下
 10月の米10年国債利回りは、一時5.3％台まで上昇したものの、足元では5.0％前後まで低下
― 月初は、原油高等によるインフレ加速懸念を受け、長期金利は約24年ぶりの高水準となる5.3％台まで上昇
― 中旬以降は、AI関連株の先行きに対する慎重な見方から、安全資産である米国債への需要が拡大し、長期金利は低下
 今後も、中東情勢に伴う原油価格の変動やAI関連株の動向など、金利を左右する不確実性が多く、振れの大きい展開が続
く可能性

国内債券・為替：株安を背景に長期金利は低下。ドル円は150円台後半で横ばい圏
 長期金利は、株安を背景に低下。10年金利・30年金利とも、10月上旬から30bps以上の大幅低下
― 米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格急落が株安の契機となり、安全資産とされる日本国債に資金が流入
― 先行き、株式市場の混乱が続けば、短期的には一段の金利低下余地も。もっとも、日銀は四半期に一度のペースで利上
げを続けるとみられ、混乱一巡後の円金利は、再び緩やかな上昇基調に転じる見通し
 ドル円は150円台後半で方向感に欠ける展開。先行きも、150円台を中心とした円安水準が続く見込み
― 原油高は円安要因の一方、株安に伴うリスクオフや仏情勢悪化を受けたユーロ安は円高要因。目先は両者が綱引きへ

内外株式：AIインフラ投資への信用不安の高まりから、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落

― 企業業績への期待から、10月初の日米株は上昇。S&P500は一時最高値を更新。日経平均も一時7万円台を回復
― その後は、米新興クラウドサービスの社債価格の急落を受け、AI・半導体関連株を中心に下落。原油価格の上昇に伴

うインフレ懸念も下押し圧力に。もっとも、S&P500はヘルスケア関連株等への資金シフトが進み、底堅く推移
 AIインフラ投資に対する信用不安は残存。原油価格の上昇も重石となり、日米株は軟調な推移が続くと予想
― 金利が高水準で推移する中、AIインフラの過剰投資懸念が強まり株価を下押しするリスクには留意が必要

## Document 083: 2308台達電 永豐

Source PDF: all report pdf/2308台達電 永豐.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

個股聚焦  
2026/11/2

財務資料 
 2025 
股東權益 (NT$百萬元) 268,058 
ROA (%) 10.26 
ROE (%) 24.13 
淨負債比率 (%) 49.13 
產業類別 電源供應器 
投資建議 買進  
收盤價 6 個月目標價 
NT$ 1335.00 NT$ 2130.00

公司簡介 
台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商 ，
為因應全球產業競爭加劇 ， 已逐步從關鍵零
組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者 ，
3Q26 營收占比：電源及零組件 (Power 
Electronics) 54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure) 33% 、 自 動 化
(Automation) 8%、交通部門  (Mobility) 
5%。近年 PC 產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積
極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動
化 、 樓宇自動化以及電動車及儲能應用 ， 由
傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域 ， 並藉由
自動化及被動元件布局提升生產效率 ， 有效
帶動獲利攀升。

主要客戶： 
主要競爭對手 ：光寶科及國外電源供應器廠
商與電源相關零組件供應商

張智皓 chihhao.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

台達電 (2308 TT) 
成長趨勢未變

永豐觀點 
3Q26 受到資料中心相關產品物料狀況不順影響，獲利不及預期，然預估4Q26 出
貨將轉順，獲利將回歸成長。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：雖近期受到料況影響獲利狀況，然台達電受惠 AI 資料中心建置需求未
變，電源及散熱零組件規格不斷提升，台達電獲利將受惠於規格持續提升，營運能
見度佳，訂單持續累積。另一方面，台達電在電力佈局持續，SOFC 解決方案將於
2027 年開始出貨。評價方面，台達電為電源及液冷產品龍頭廠商市占率高，受惠
程度明顯，營運將進入成長期，故以2027 年稅後 EPS 56.88 元計算，目前本益比
約 26 倍，處於歷史評價區間下緣，考量台達電逐漸從過往的零組件供應商轉型成
為系統整合商的角色，有利於在產品市占及獲利能力的提升，且獲利成長性顯著，
故維持買進建議，目標價 2130 元(30X 2027-2028 平均 EPS)。

ESG 評析 
台達電企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 B+等與 A+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。
SinoPac Securities Investment Service licenses and applies the SASB Materiality Map® 
General Issue Categories and Disclosure Topics in our work.

### Page 2

2

台達電為全球 Power 龍頭廠商：台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商，為因
應全球產業競爭加劇，已逐步從關鍵零組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者，
3Q26 營 收 占 比 ： 電 源 及 零 組 件(Power Electronics)54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure)33%、 自動化(Automation)8%、交通部門(Mobility)5%。 近年PC
產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動化、樓宇自動
化以及電動車及儲能應用， 由傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域， 並藉由自動化及
被動元件布局提升生產效率，有效帶動獲利攀升。主要競爭對手為光寶科及國外電
源供應器廠商與電源相關零組件供應商。 
資料中心相關業務仍為成長主軸 ：電源及零組件部門(Power Electronics)部門營收
1059.3 億(+10% QoQ，+40% YoY)，成長主要來自資料中心電源及液冷出貨，營
業利益 199.2 億(-1% QoQ，+22% YoY)，營利率 18.8%；交通(Mobility) 部門營
收 90.7 億(+10% QoQ，+7% YoY)，受電動車需求持續疲弱，營業損失 1.37 億；
自動化(Automation)部門營收 159 億(+2% QoQ，+19% YoY)，因物料轉順，營
業利益 7.69 億元 ， 營利率4.8%； 基礎設施(Infrastructure)部門營收 658.8 億(+5% 
QoQ，+25% YoY)，成長來自資料中心液冷產品持續出貨，營業利益 120.68 億，
營利率 18.3%。整體來說，台達電主要動能來自於高階伺服器電源及散熱相關產
品，在自動化略有復甦，交通部門虧損收斂。 
供應鏈料況及資料中心建置遞延影響，3Q26 獲利不及預期：台達電 3Q26 營收
1970.59 億元(+7.5%QoQ，+31.1%YoY)，因長短料影響毛利率約 1.7%，毛利率
34.1%(-1.5ptsQoQ，-0.8ptsYoY)， 不及預期的36%， 營利率16.6%(-0.1ptsQoQ，
+0.1ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 234.95 億元(-6.5%QoQ，+26.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 9.05
元，低於預期的 9.98 元。 
4Q26 電源及散熱產品出貨暢旺：受惠於 AI 伺服器需求持續持續增加，先前遞延
訂單將出貨，研究部上修預估 4Q26 營收 2162.3 億元(+9.7%QoQ，+33.8%YoY)，
在價格調漲及部分庫存迴轉，毛利率35.5%(+1.4pts QoQ，+0.9ptsYoY)，營利率
18.5%(+1.9pts QoQ ，+2.2ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 296.47 億元 (+26.2%QoQ，
+71.1%YoY)， 稅後EPS 為 11.41 元 。 預估2026 年營收 7588.99 億元(+36.2%YoY)，
毛利率 35.5%(+1.2ptsYoY)，營利率 17.4%(+2.3ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 988.33 億元
(+64.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 38.05 元。 
AI 零組件產品內容價值提升及規模效應下，台達電成長快速：展望未來，電源產
品方面，隨著伺服器對電源需求增加，Vera Rubin 單顆 PSU 18.3kW 產品已經出
貨。此外，正負 400V DC 電源產品已小量出貨，預估將於 2H27 量產，成為未來

主要成長動能之一 。 電源ACDC 電源模組產品潛在市場持續擴大將繼續成長 ，DC-
DC 產品需求明顯回溫。散熱方面，液對氣維持高市佔率，且整體市場潛在產值仍
持續擴大，營利率亦高於公司平均，獲利貢獻顯著。整體而言，台達電在 AI 伺服
器規格提升下，成為主要受益的廠商之一，且因台達電具備關鍵零組件自製的優
勢 ， 能滿足客戶需求及控制成本 ， 長期競爭力佳 。研究部預估 2027 年營收 1.03 兆
元(+36.4% YoY)，毛利率 36.4%(+0.9pts YoY)，在規模經濟效應持續擴大下，營
利率 19.5%(+2.1pts YoY)，稅後淨利 1477.5 億元(+49.5% YoY)，稅後 EPS 為
56.88 元。預估2028 年在 800VDC 電源櫃產品及散熱產品持續放量，且燃料電池
及儲能系統將陸續貢獻營收，預估台達電2028 年營收 1.4 兆元(+36.7% YoY)，毛
利率 37.2%(+0.8pts YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 85.21 元。

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部維持台達電買進投資建議，主係：(1) 3Q26受缺料影響出貨
遞延，並認列存貨跌價損失，但漲價效益將自4Q26起逐步反映，延
續至1Q27，毛利率可望逐步回升，營運最壞情況已過；(2)資料中
心相關營收占比已達五成，液冷今年占比約13%，全年營收可望突
破900億元，明年仍維持成長；(3) 缺電與缺料雖然影響資料中心
建置節奏，但管理層表示訂單能見度仍高，主要是出貨時點遞延，
需求並未消失；(4) 股價回檔幅度已大，評價已明顯回落。綜上所
述，研究部微幅下修27年營收1.04兆元(原估1.05兆元)，EPS下修
至60.37元(原估62.68元)。評價方面，考量HVDC滲透率仍不明朗，
僅給予31倍本益比，目標價由2,400元下修至1,870元。整體而言，
營運最壞情況已過且股價回檔已多，維持買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26受缺料干擾，毛利率承壓但OPM大致持穩 
3Q26營收1,971億元(原估2,102億元)， 季增7.5%， 年增31.1%，
營收達成率93.7%，低於預期，主因是資料中心客戶長短料、
建置延遲，造成出貨遞延。毛利率降至34.1%(原估35.4%)，季
減1.6pts， 低於預期 ， 主係因出貨遞延公司因此認列約39億元
存貨跌價損失(約影響1.7%，涉及產品非VR相關產品，研究部
推測為ASIC產品)，加上漲價在3Q26幾乎還沒反映。不過費用
控管得宜，營利率16.6%(原估16.9%)，僅季減0.1pts，本業獲
利能力大致持穩 。 業外收益受股市評價影響 ， 由2Q26的44億元
降至10億元，EPS為9.04元(原估10.54元)，低於預期。

2. 4Q26毛利率可望止跌回升，營運低點已過 
由於缺料尚未完全解除，研究部微幅調整4Q26營收至2,248億
元(原估2,253億元)，但季增14.1%、年增39.1%，成長動能仍
強。獲利方面，公司表示4Q毛利率優於3Q，主係存貨跌價損失
新增認列有望減少 、 開始有較多新價格體現， 以及電源業務成
長 帶 動 產 品 組 合 改 善 ， 故 研 究 部 預 估 毛 利 率34.8%( 原估
35.8%)， 季增0.7pts； 營利率17.2%(原估18.2%)， 季增0.6pts；
EPS下修至10.14元(原估11.03元)。展望1Q27，研究部預估營
收2,308億元，季增2.6%，表現優於以往的淡季季節性，主要
是部分AI遞延訂單可望延續至1Q27出貨。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. 資料中心營收占比過半，訂單能見度仍高 
管理層表示 ， 今年資料中心相關營收已占總營收一半 ，冷卻系
統約占13%，液冷3Q營收約250億元，26年營收可望突破900億
元 ， 且公司對1H27液冷需求看法仍偏樂觀 。雖然缺電影響部分
資料中心的建置進度 ，但管理層強調訂單能見度高， 變化主要
是出貨時點的延後，並非需求消失。此外，研究部認為，台達
電在供應鏈管理上具備優勢， 隨著缺料逐漸緩解 ，遞延的需求
可望陸續轉為營收。

4. 然Rubin Ultra機櫃出現低壓方案，HVDC滲透率仍待觀察 
HVDC方面 ， 正負400V進展較快 ， 已開始出貨 ；800V則由NV主推 ，
進度較慢。根據供應鏈調查，Ultra平台目前已有低壓設計方
案，若TDP增幅不大，HVDC的省電效益相對有限，客戶未必非
用800V不可，因此研究部下修2027年營收預估。此外，公司已
通過馬來西亞檳城新據點的投資案 ， 為中長期AI電源需求預先
擴充產能。在Vicor相關訴訟上，仍在ITC調查程序中，短期對
出貨不會有影響，公司對自身IP技術具有信心。

5. 評價已回落，維持買進 
綜上所述，研究部下修2026年營收至7,645億元(原估7,781億
元)，年增37.8%；毛利率35.3%(原估35.9%)；EPS下修至36.77
元(原估39.16元)。 同步下修2027年營收至1.04兆元(原估1.05
兆元)， 年增36.4%； 毛利率36.1%(原估36.5%)；EPS下修至60.37
元(原估62.68元)，年增64.2%。考量HVDC滲透率仍不明朗，僅
給予31倍本益比， 目標價由2,400元下修至1,870元 。 惟股價評
價已明顯回落，且營運最壞情況已過，維持買進建議。

## Document 179: 261105_ms_nypcb

### Page 1

M
Update
Nan Ya PCB  | Asia Pacific
In-line 3Q26 gross margin
3Q26 results:
• 3Q revenue was NT$16.4bn (+21% q/q, +49% y/y).
• GM reached 29.3% (+460bps q/q, +1,970bps y/y), slightly above our estimate
but in line with the Street, driven by higher ABF (low 30s  %) and BT GM
(high 20s  %).  PCB remains  loss-making on the gross margin level.
• OP was NT$4.3bn (+49% q/q, +597% y/y), 1% above our estimate and the
Street, for OpM of 26.4% (+500bps q/q, +2,070bps y/y).

• However, non-op income was below our estimate and the Street. Tax rate of
~26% was again higher than our estimate at 16% and the Street at 19%.
• Thus, NP came in at NT$3.3bn (+46% q/q, +355% y/y), 13% below our
estimate/8% below the Street, for EPS of  NT$5.10.

Key Takeaways
NYPCB's 3Q GM is  largely in line with both buy-side and sell-side estimates.
The 500bps sequential improvement in OpM looks positive to us. Similar to

Unimicron's 3Q results, we view NYPCB's slight miss as an expectation
mismatch...
...rather than reflecting any structural concerns.
We also expect NYPCB to gain share for ABF substrates for AMD's server CPUs,
as one Japanese competitor reallocates more capacity to another CPU customer.
We remain constructive on all ABF substrate stocks in our coverage.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers

n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity
Nan Ya PCB (8046.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate

value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.3%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 17%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF and BT demand
n Faster-than-expected pickup in AI and 5G demand
n Stronger-than-expected ASP hike
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.

CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall, creating headwinds for ABF substrate demand and pricing
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition

STOCK RATINGS

Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 190: 5.7万亿美元“灰飞烟灭”！一场“应已计价”的中选却引爆了“科技股灾”

Source PDF: all report pdf/5.7万亿美元“灰飞烟灭”！一场“应已计价”的中选却引爆了“科技股灾”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-07
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⺠主党⼤胜国会后不⾜ 24 ⼩时，华尔街上演惊天逆转：纳斯达克 100 创 2020 年 3 ⽉以来最⼤
单⽇跌幅， VIX 恐慌指数从历史平静基点暴飙近 70% ，美股整体市值⾃ 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇⾼点算累计
损失已达 5.7 万亿美元。散⼾夺路⽽逃、对冲基⾦跟⻛踩踏、量化策略机械平仓 —— 三路同
时砸盘， AI 板块遭遇清算式重定价，积累已久的泡沫忧虑，在⼀场选举结果⾯前骤然引
爆。
摘要：
积累已久的 AI 泡沫忧虑，在⼀场选举结果⾯前骤然引爆。
美国中期选举⺠主党⼤胜国会，本应是⼀个 " ⻛险出清 " 的信号 —— 股市最初也如此反应。但不到
24 ⼩时，华尔街完成了从庆祝到恐慌的戏剧性翻转。周四，纳斯达克 100 指数录得 2020 年 3 ⽉以
来最⼤单⽇跌幅，单⽇蒸发市值 2.3 万亿美元；⾃ 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇⾼点计算，美国股市累计市值损失已
达 5.7 万亿美元。 VIX 恐慌指数从 16.6 点飙升⾄近 28 点，创下史上从最平静起点出发、升幅最为
剧烈的波动事件之⼀。

导⽕索是市场对⺠主党政策⽴场的重新定价 —— 尤其是后者对 AI 数据中⼼⼤规模扩张的深度质
疑。这⼀政治变量叠加早已⾼度拥挤的 AI 交易仓位，形成了散⼾、对冲基⾦、量化策略三路同时
踩踏的罕⻅景象。这场抛售的破坏⼒之所以如此惊⼈，在于它⼏乎没有给任何⼈留下反应时
间，从散⼾率先出逃，到对冲基⾦跟进，再到量化策略机械性平仓 —— 在短短数⼩时内完成。
选举夜：从庆祝到困惑
周⼆夜间，⺠主党的压倒性胜势超出预期。据华尔街⽇报报道，投资研究机构负责⼈ Ben
Emons 坐在曼哈顿办公室，从晚上 9 点到午夜，电话铃声⼏乎没有停过。他⼀边接听客⼾来电，
⼀边在桌边做俯卧撑提神。 " ⼈们被打了个措⼿不及， " 他说， " 这是⼀个真正的冲击事件。 "
彼时，股指期货在初步计票时⼀度下滑，但随着⺠主党胜势清晰、特朗普公开表⽰接受选举结
果，期货迅速翻红。 Key Private Bank ⾸席投资官 George Mateyo 在家中边看直播边与同事发短
信，他的判断是：选举后出现激烈争议的⻛险已经消散，市场理应对此表⽰欢迎。

这⼀判断在周三上午得到了印证。建筑、零售等 " 实体经济 " 板块⼤涨，道指录得 4 ⽉以来最佳单
⽇表现。华尔街短暂地松了⼀⼝⽓ —— 以为选举的不确定性已经⼀笔勾销。
然⽽，这场反弹很快被证明是⼀次假突破。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

散⼾率先出逃，恐慌情绪蔓延社交媒体
周四的抛售，最先由散⼾点燃。
开盘后的头半⼩时，纳斯达克指数急速下挫，个⼈投资者的净卖出规模创下历史性纪录。据分
析机构 Vanda Research 数据，周四单⽇散⼾净卖出规模仅次于 2020 年 3 ⽉新冠疫情冲击期间的
两个交易⽇。摩根⼤通市场情报部⻔则指出，散⼾卖出量偏离正常⽔平逾三个标准差。
" 我早上 7 点就开始⼯作了， " 哥伦⽐亚⼤学⼯程系研究⽣、哥伦⽐亚期权与期货交易俱乐部成员
Raphael Monges 说， " 我看到市场开盘，纳斯达克跌得⻜快，然后是标普。纳斯达克太疯狂
了，所有 AI 股都在砸盘。我的⼿机开始不停震动，所有⼈都在盯着股票，全是红⾊。 "
旧⾦⼭投资者兼内容创作者 Kevin Xu 在周四上午 10 点半瞥了⼀眼⾏情，随即愣住了。纳斯达克
已经跌了 4% ，他的退休账⼾⼀夜之间蒸发了六位数。 " 什么都做不了， " 他说， " 只能看着。 " 在他
的社交媒体信息流⾥，其他散⼾投资者有的发表情包，有的⽤全⼤写字⺟发泄情绪，还有⼈贴

出了⼼理健康热线的电话号码。
加州 50 岁的散⼾交易者 Joel Rieger 没有在这波动荡中调整仓位。 " 昨天的投资组合遭受了沉重打
击，到处都是红⾊， " 他说， " 短期内会很痛。 "
对冲基⾦跟进，机构抛售⽆⼈接盘
散⼾的出逃只是序幕。随着市场⽅向确⽴，对冲基⾦和⻓线资产管理⼈相继加⼊抛售⾏列，⽽
另⼀侧⼏乎没有买家出现。
据彭博社报道， " 机构投资者已经退场，⽽散⼾和系统性策略正在激进抛售， "Vanda 全球宏观策
略师 Viraj Patel 在报告中写道， " 这种资⾦流动态势，在真正的市场压⼒时期之外极为罕⻅。 "
摩根⼤通全球股票⾼接触交易联席主管 Ron Adler 在现⾦交易台⽬睹了全程 ——当⽇交易量较平
时激增 50% 。 " 我们看到的部分是投资组合的痛苦和⽆差别抛售，" 他在给客⼾的报告中写
道， " 今天资⾦流向的坦率程度在⽅向上是合理的，但规模之⼤令⼈咋⾆。 "
据⾼盛 Prime 经纪台估算，基本⾯多空对冲基⾦周四单⽇亏损 1.3% ，属于两个标准差事件；科技

主题基⾦跌幅超过 4% ，年初⾄今收益⼏乎归零。动量策略遭受了 3.7 个标准差的损失。摩根⼠丹
利数据则显⽰，对冲基⾦总杠杆率⾃ 6 ⽉峰值以来的降幅，已是⼗余年来最⼤。
量化平仓接⼒，系统性卖压⾃我强化
当市场跌势确⽴，⼀系列量化交易机制被触发，将抛售压⼒推向新的量级。
野村证券跨资产策略董事总经理 Charlie McElligott 估算，周四盘中期权交易商的负 Gamma 敞⼝
约为每 1% 市场波动对应 70 亿美元 —— 即股市每下跌 1% ，交易商就需额外卖出 70 亿美元股票以

### Page 3

对冲⻛险。与此同时，波动率控制基⾦需要抛售约 610 亿美元股票，其中 250 亿美元来⾃周三尚
未完成的平仓；杠杆 ETF 的收盘再平衡则将再贡献约 470 亿美元的卖盘。
趋势跟踪量化基⾦（ CTA ）也在同步撤退。据 Vanda 估算， CTA 在标普 500 和纳斯达克期货上合
计卖出约 130 亿美元，持仓降⾄历史低位附近。
巴克莱估计，波动率控制基⾦单⽇削减股票敞⼝达 30 个百分点，为 2020 年 2 ⽉新冠疫情冲击以来
最 ⼤ 单 ⽇ 降 幅 。 " 与 客 ⼾ 的 对 话 告 诉 我 ， 他 们 在 这 个 时 点 进 ⼀ 步 降 低 ⻛ 险 的 动 ⼒ 更 强
了， "McElligott 写道， " 反馈的主题是：保住年初⾄今剩余的⼀点点收益，活着进⼊明年的新资
本和新⻛险周期。 "
下午 3 点，纳斯达克跌幅已达 4.5% ，部分交易员还在期待最后⼀⼩时的反弹。结果抛售进⼀步加
剧，标普 500 指数收于当⽇低点附近，录得去年 4 ⽉以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。
AI 板块遭到清算，损伤集中⽽深重

这场抛售的破坏⼒⾼度集中于 AI 相关板块。
存储芯⽚、光⽹络、数据中⼼及 AI 半导体等细分板块单⽇跌幅达 12% ⾄ 15% ，属于五⾄六个标
准差的极端波动。约⼗余个 AI 股票篮⼦均创下⾃ 2022 年 11 ⽉ ChatGPT 发布以来的最差单⽇表
现。其中跌幅最重的存储和光通信篮⼦，已较 6 ⽉⾼点腰斩，跌幅超过过去四年任何⼀次回调。
截⾄周四收盘，纳斯达克 100 指数成分股中有 57% 较⾼点跌逾 20% ，这⼀⽐例甚⾄⾼于 2022 年 3
⽉该指数本⾝进⼊熊市时的⽔平。
这场崩盘之所以显得格外戏剧性，部分原因在于它⼏乎从平静中骤然爆发。 VIX 在周四开盘时仅
为 16.6 点，低于⼀年均值；收盘时已接近 28 点。⾃ 1980 年以来，标普 500 在前⼀⽇上涨 1.5% 后
次⽇下跌 2% 以上的情形仅出现过 48 次，⼏乎全部发⽣在熊市或危机期间。
政治冲击叠加旧忧， AI 叙事⾯临重新定价
市场的剧烈反应，折射出投资者对 AI 投资逻辑的深层焦虑。
在选举之前，市场已对 AI 交易的可持续性存有疑虑：科技巨头资本开⽀持续膨胀、现⾦储备收

缩、数据中⼼运营商资产负债表上债务堆积。⺠主党的胜选则叠加了⼀层新的政治⻛险 —— ⺠
主党对数据中⼼扩张的强烈抵制，尤其是在德克萨斯、俄亥俄、佐治亚等已成为数据中⼼建设
关键战场的州，令 AI 基础设施建设的前景蒙上阴影。
美银分析师 Michael Hartnett 指出，在受访选⺠中，⾼达 76% 的⼈反对在⾃⼰社区附近新建数据
中⼼，这正成为 AI 基础设施扩张的严峻障碍。
Man Group ⾼级投资组合经理 Matt Rowe 表⽰，选举结果提供了 " 可量化的证据，证明公众对数
据中⼼建设存在真实的反感 " ，这 " 开始改变我对这些项⽬落地概率的判断 " 。 " 所有条件不变的情

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

况下，这些项⽬将需要更⻓时间推进，成本也将更⾼。 "
Causeway Capital Management 投资组合经理 Joe Gubler 指出， " 总会有输家，并⾮每个押注在
资本⽀出上的⼈都能成为赢家。 "
另⼀边，提前离场的投资者则避开了这场⻛暴。 Jeff Muhlenkamp 在暴跌前⼏乎清空了基⾦内全
部 AI 持仓，其规模 2.75 亿美元的投资组合 11 ⽉以来基本持平，全年跑赢标普 500 。 " 我们卖掉了
昂贵⽽流⾏的，买⼊了便宜⽽被遗忘的， " 他说， " 当动量转向，我们受益。 "
市场寻找出路
在 AI 交易遭到清算的同时，投资者正在寻找新的避⻛港 —— 但这条路并不平坦。
早在选举之前，资⾦已开始向公⽤事业、必需消费品等估值更低的防御性板块轮动。 10 ⽉份，

成⻓股跑输价值股达 10 个百分点，为 2001 年以来最⼤单⽉差距。然⽽周四的暴跌表明，这些新
宠板块同样未能抵御科技股崩盘的冲击波。
部分投资者则提前布局，得以全⾝⽽退。基⾦经理 Jeff Muhlenkamp 在暴跌前已清仓⼏乎所有 AI
持仓，其 2.75 亿美元的投资组合 11 ⽉基本持平，年初⾄今跑赢标普 500 。 " 我们卖掉了贵的、热
⻔的，买⼊了便宜的、被遗忘的， " 他说， " 当动量转向，我们受益。 "
周五，芯⽚股率先反弹，纳斯达克 100 指数⼀度回升逾 2.6% ，但各主要股指全周仍以下跌收
场，标普 500 周跌幅为 2.68% 。 Bright Trading 的资深交易员 Sinisa Sorgic ，当天做空台积电美
股，却因后者盘中反弹逾 7.8% ⽽ " 被烧伤 " 。他总结这⼀周的感受：" 昨天是⼀场⾎战。现在市场
太疯狂了，根本不敢全⼒冲进去抄底。 "
市场分歧已然浮现。 Key Private Bank 的 Mateyo 认为，州级法规会延缓、但不会阻⽌数据中⼼

的建设，公众对 AI 的疑虑或将随着实际使⽤的普及⽽逐步消退。 Wellington Management 的
Casaletto 则态度审慎，认为 AI 扩张速度放缓的趋势已经确⽴。
这场⻛暴究竟是 AI ⽜市中期的⼀次剧烈震荡，还是更深层周期转换的开端，华尔街尚⽆定论。但
⼀个现实已经清晰：当政治⻛向与拥挤仓位同时转向，市场所能提供的缓冲，远⽐想象中要薄
得多。
请发表您的评论

## Document 217: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다SK하이닉스(000660) 여전히 강력하다여전히 강력하다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

00

SK 하이닉스 (000660/KS) 
여전히 강력하다 
3Q26 영업이익 83 조원으로 예상 상회 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 83조원 (+38% QoQ)으로 예상을 상회했다. 메모
리 공급 부족 심화, HBM4 반영 시작에 따른 메모리 가격 상승이 환율 하락을 상쇄했
기 때문이다. 출하 DRAM +10%, NAND -1%, 가격 DRAM +28%, NAND 
+24%, 영업이익률 DRAM 82%, NAND 72%, HBM 판가는 HBM4 출하가 시작되
며 37% 수준 상승한 것으로 추정된다. 업황은 여전히 예상을 상회하고 있다.

4Q26 분기 영업이익 100 조원 시대 진입 전망 
4Q26 영업이익은 105조원 (+27% QoQ)으로 분기 100조원 시대에 진입할 것으로 
전망한다. 메모리 가격 강세가 지속되는 가운데, 1c 출하 가속화, M15X 램프업 본격화 
등으로 출하량 상승이 기존 예상을 상회할 것이다. 또한 HBM4의 Cross-over 시작 
및 수율 안정화로 HBM 가격, 수익성 제고 동반이 지속될 것이다. 출하는 DRAM 
+24%, NAND +4%, 가격은 DRAM +8%, NAND +7%를 예상한다.

2027 년 영업이익 488 조원 (+70% YoY)으로 상향조정 
SK하이닉스의 2027년 영업이익을 488조원 (+70% YoY)으로 12% 상향 조정한
다. 원/달러 환율 전망치를 기존 1,340원에서 1,315원으로 하향 조정했음에도, 공급 
부족 심화 속 2027년향 HBM 가격 인상 및 예상을 상회하는 수율, 1Q27 진행될 모바
일 DRAM 가격 인상 등을 반영했기 때문이다. LTA 가격 역시 개별 약정된 범위 내 
시장 상황을 반영할 수 있는 구조라는 점을 감안하면, 지속 상승할 것이라 전망한다.

높은 주주환원 의지 확인, 높아진 실적 가시성, 지나친 저평가 
투자의견 매수, 목표주가 4,000,000원을 유지한다. SK하이닉스는 LTA에 대해 기
존 5년에서 추가 연장에 대한 논의가 진행 중이며, 2029년까지 HBM, DRAM은 사실
상 완판, HBM 수요는 공급 가능 물량의 2 배를 상회, 2030년까지 메모리 수요 우위 
국면 전망을 밝혔다. AI 인프라 투자의 강력한 명분과 메모리 수요의 구조성, 잠재적으
로 매우 높은 LTA 위반 비용을 감안하면 실적 가시성과 안정성 제고가 동반되는 국면
이다. 100조원 수준의 주주환원은 2026년 예상 FCF의 66% 수준, 분기별 자사주 
매입, 소각 지속 계획을 통해 높은 주주환원 의지를 보여주고 있다. 실적 Duration 장
기화, 주주환원을 감안하면 12MF P/E 3.4X 수준의 현 주가는 현저한 저평가 국면이
다. 과도한 우려의 되돌림과 메모리 밸류에 대한 재논의 국면 진입을 전망한다.

2026-10-29

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

## Document 254: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적HD현대중공업(329180) 실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발_5484f7f5.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82만원에서 64만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

## Document 344: France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s social demands run up against tightening fiscal constraints.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Thursday’s nationwide mobilisation over wages and purchasing
power will add pressure on a French government already
struggling with weak growth, a fragile labour market and an
increasingly unmanageable budget. Its options for responding
to social discontent are narrowing as higher borrowing costs
further weaken the outlook for public debt
Charlotte de Montpellier
Student-led protests have grown into a broader social movement, putting
more pressure on the French government

Pressure mounts for action on purchasing
power
What began as a student-led protest has grown into a broader
social movement, adding to the government’s challenges as it
struggles to get its 2027 budget through parliament.
The mobilisation continued during the school holidays, with
between 320,000 and 500,000 people taking to the streets last
Saturday, fewer than in previous weeks but hardly the pause the

### Page 2

government had hoped for. The next test will come on Thursday, 5
November, when all the main trade unions will mobilise over wages
and purchasing power, with students and public sector workers
expected to join the protests.
Under pressure, the government is considering raising the
minimum wage by more than the automatic increase of around
1% due in January, possibly adding another 0.5 to 0.6 percentage
points. This would be the ﬁrst discretionary increase since 2012. But

employers are strongly opposed, as they are already facing higher
labour costs through planned cuts to social contribution
exemptions. A further rise in the minimum wage could therefore
encourage companies to limit recruitment or reduce their
workforce, at a time when the labour market is already fragile.
Private sector employment has declined for seven consecutive
quarters in annual terms and now stands 0.3% lower than a year
ago, representing a loss of almost 70,000 jobs. A discretionary

increase in the minimum wage would also have implications for the
public sector. It could raise the pay of around 15% of public sector
employees, adding further pressure on the public ﬁnances.
Part of the additional wage cost could also be passed on to
consumers, adding to inﬂationary pressures. Higher energy prices
have already pushed headline inﬂation from 3.1% in September to
an estimated 3.4% in October, while harmonised inﬂation reached
3.8%.

Budget talks are producing a larger deﬁcit,
not a solution
These pressures are making an already diﬃcult budget equation
even harder to solve. After 10 full days of debate and more than
2,300 amendments examined, France’s 2027 budget discussions
appear to be going nowhere, except towards a larger deﬁcit than
the government intended. While the government aims to reduce
the deﬁcit to 5% of GDP in 2027, after an expected 5.4% in 2026,
the measures approved so far have pushed it substantially further

oﬀ course. According to the Finance Ministry, the amendments
adopted during the ﬁrst reading have already worsened the

### Page 3

projected deﬁcit by €23.9bn (total deﬁcit in 2026 will be around
€165bn).
The vote on the revenue part has been postponed again, as MPs
continue to adopt measures pointing in all directions. Yet the
exercise increasingly makes little sense: the fragmented National
Assembly is unlikely to approve the budget, leaving the
government with two possible escape routes. It could use Article
49.3 to pass the bill without a vote, but would ﬁrst need to
negotiate with parts of the opposition to ensure that a subsequent

no-conﬁdence motion fails. For now, there are few signs of such
talks. If the motion passed, the government would fall and the
budget would be rejected. Alternatively, once the constitutional 70-
day deadline expires on 18 December, the government could
implement its original budget by ordinance. A two-stage solution,
with the ordinance followed by a corrective bill, is reportedly
gaining support among MPs, including some Socialists. The
corrective bill could accommodate selected social promises.

Weak growth makes the ﬁscal targets
unrealistic
The problem is that the government’s initial budget will no longer
be suﬃcient to bring the deﬁcit down to 5% of GDP. It was based on
GDP growth of 1% in 2027, an assumption that now looks clearly
too optimistic. The French economy contracted by 0.1% in the third
quarter, after stagnating in the second and shrinking by 0.2% in the
ﬁrst, leaving GDP marginally lower than a year earlier and France
hovering close to recession.

The details of the third-quarter ﬁgure were also weak: investment
fell by 0.6% and net trade subtracted 0.6 percentage points from
growth as exports dropped sharply, while inventories provided a
temporary oﬀset. The only real positive surprise came from
household consumption, which rose by 0.4%. But there is little
reason to expect this momentum to last. Consumer conﬁdence fell
by ﬁve points to 80 in October, its lowest level since June 2013 and
well below its long-term average of 100. Households’ assessment

of whether this is a good time to make major purchases dropped

### Page 4

sharply, while fears of unemployment increased. Most strikingly,
their assessment of living standards fell to its lowest level since the
survey began in October 1972. This points to much weaker
consumption in the coming months.
The outlook for investment is equally weak. Financing conditions
are deteriorating particularly sharply in France: according to the
latest ECB Bank Lending Survey, a net 27% of French banks
tightened credit standards for businesses in the third quarter,

compared with around 8% in Germany, no tightening in Spain and
an easing in Italy. Together with weak demand, higher interest
rates and uncertainty over taxes and public spending, this is likely
to encourage companies and households to postpone projects.
Combined with political uncertainty and social tensions, growth is
likely to be very weak in the fourth quarter as well. All in all, we now
expect GDP growth of just 0.3% this year and 0.5% in 2027, with

risks still tilted to the downside. Under these assumptions,
implementing the budget unchanged would leave the deﬁcit at
around 5.5% of GDP. Reaching the government’s 5% target would
require roughly €12bn in additional savings, on top of the €43bn
eﬀort already included in the budget, which looks highly unlikely in
the current political environment. And that is before including the
almost €500mn cost of the measures promised to high-school and
university students.

Debt is unlikely to stabilise anytime soon
All this leaves France’s debt dynamics looking increasingly
uncomfortable. The government had expected public debt to reach
121.7% of GDP in 2027, already a level at which a 5% deﬁcit would
be insuﬃcient to stabilise the debt ratio. With growth now likely to
be weaker and the deﬁcit higher than assumed in the budget, debt
will probably rise even further, and there is little prospect of
stabilisation anytime soon.

The sharp rise in borrowing costs only makes the arithmetic worse.
The French 10-year yield is currently around 4.9%, more than
155bp above its German equivalent, compared with a spread of
around 70bp at the end of 2025. Admittedly, the increase in market
Cookie settings

### Page 5

yields feeds only gradually into the eﬀective cost of the debt as
existing bonds mature: the implicit interest rate on French public
debt is still around 2.2% in 2026. But this also means that a
substantial part of the rise in interest costs has yet to come
through. Fitch estimates that France would need to reduce its
primary deﬁcit from around 2.9% of GDP this year to just 0.4% to
stabilise the debt ratio. The adjustment required would become
considerably larger if the eﬀective interest rate on the debt

gradually converged towards current market yields.
And the 2027 presidential election is unlikely to provide an easy
reset. A new president could again ﬁnd her or himself without a
parliamentary majority, making the large and sustained ﬁscal
adjustment required increasingly diﬃcult to deliver. These concerns
prompted Fitch to take the unusual step on 30 October of bringing
forward its assessment and revising the outlook on France’s A+
rating from stable to negative, only a week after Moody’s

unexpectedly kept its Aa3 rating unchanged, albeit with a negative
outlook. Fitch explicitly pointed to weaker growth, higher borrowing
costs and a wider deﬁcit, together with political fragmentation and
social tensions that make sustained ﬁscal consolidation harder to
achieve. It expects French public debt to reach around 128% of GDP
by 2030, considerably above the roughly 123% projected by the
IMF.
All in all, tomorrow’s demonstrations will once again underline the

population’s frustration over purchasing power. But without
stronger economic growth to lift household incomes while
simultaneously easing the pressure on the public ﬁnances, the
government has very limited room to oﬀer measures large enough
to make a meaningful diﬀerence without making an already
worrying ﬁscal trajectory even worse.

## Document 353: How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres

Source PDF: all report pdf/How the blue wave could reshape US energy and data centres.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy aﬀordability shaped the midterms. A divided
government will now face pressure to shield consumers from
rising data centre power costs while speeding up new energy
supply. Onshoring and trade protections will continue, despite
higher near-term costs, as energy security remains a priority
Coco Zhang
After the midterm elections, the government will need to minimise data
centre-related costs for consumers, bring new power supply online faster and

protect supply chains. Pictured: Data centre in Ohio
Exit polls from the US midterm elections highlighted a key concern
shaping voters’ decisions: energy aﬀordability. Nearly 90% of
voters said the cost of living was a crucial or major factor in their
vote for Congress, with many pointing to gasoline and utility costs.
Meanwhile, as the AI data centre boom strains power supply and
infrastructure, 56% said the issue was a crucial or major factor in

their vote.
With Democrats now set to control both the House and Senate,
what does this mean for US energy and data centre policy?

### Page 2

Newly elected lawmakers will face pressure to address these
concerns, requiring the Democratic Congress and Republican White
House to ﬁnd common ground on shielding consumers from data
centre-related costs and accelerating new power supply, including
through permitting reform. Onshoring and supply chain protections
are also likely to continue. While they can raise near-term costs,
they remain important to US energy security. Developments in all
three areas could have signiﬁcant implications for businesses.

That said, Congress needs to strike a balance when drafting
legislation aimed at achieving these goals. More aggressive
measures could face a veto from President Trump.
Strengthened data centre regulation ahead,
but the devil is in the detail
The data centre industry is facing heightened local opposition.
Recent polls suggest that around one in seven Americans is
concerned about or opposes data centre development in their
neighbourhoods.
In response to this issue, there is likely to be tighter data centre

regulation. State-level policy will be more important to watch, as
action is politically and practically easier at this level. The most
likely approach is targeted regulation, much of which is already
emerging. This includes data centre-related taxes, reduced tax
incentives, environmental standards, and requirements for
developers to bear relevant cost increases. Outright moratoriums
remain unlikely to become mainstream. Where they are introduced,

they are more likely to be temporary, giving states time to develop
a data centre policy framework.
At the federal level, the key unanswered question is how much
authority should remain with the states and whether federal
legislation should mandate state regulations or simply provide
guidance. Republicans generally favour letting states lead, while
Democrats tend to support a stronger federal role.
We think sweeping data centre legislation is unlikely because of

President Trump’s veto power. Federal action is more likely to focus

on aﬀordability by protecting electricity customers from higher
costs and formalising data centres' responsibility to help fund grid
and energy infrastructure upgrades. Such measures could be added
to broader legislation, such as must-pass spending bills needed to
avoid a government shutdown, or a bipartisan package like
permitting reform.
More broadly on AI, we expect the Democratic Party to increase
scrutiny of technology companies, as discussed here, while utilising

the next two years to prepare an AI governance framework for 2028
and beyond.
However, tighter AI and data centre regulation is unlikely to
fundamentally alter the industry’s growth trajectory. AI’s
importance to US economic growth and competition with China will
remain powerful drivers of expansion.
Business implications
State-level policy would more directly aﬀect project siting,
which is important for data centre companies to watch.
Where state rules are tighter, project development could slow.

However, a more restrictive regulatory environment could
also increase the value of data centres already approved or
under construction, easing some of the industry’s capital
constraints. Higher valuations could also encourage
developers to be more selective and expand at a more
measured pace, ultimately improving the quality of the
project pipeline.
Developers should also treat community engagement as a
strategic investment. Recent surveys show that while only

20-30% of Americans support local data centre construction,
support can rise to 50-70% when there are accompanying
community investments, such as in public infrastructure
improvements or reductions in residents' costs. More
developers are now signing binding community beneﬁt
agreements as part of project development.

Enabling faster power generation through
permitting reform
Both parties support timely expansion of electricity supply to meet

rising demand from AI and manufacturing reshoring. They also
generally agree that energy abundance can help reduce cost
pressures. While Democrats and Republicans diﬀer on the preferred
energy mix, there is still common ground. Technologies likely to
retain bipartisan support include nuclear, geothermal, carbon
capture, and biofuels.
However, the greatest potential for policy action may lie not in
speciﬁc technologies, but in permitting reform.
Today, many energy projects can spend over ﬁve years navigating

the federal permitting process. Around $240bn to $280bn of
infrastructure investment across the eight sectors below enters the

With both parties recognising the need for reform, the Senate
introduced the Bipartisan American Aﬀordability and Jobs Act
(BAAJA) in September 2026. Key permitting proposals include:

Speeding up approvals for energy projects
Providing greater certainty and consistency across
administrations
Making it easier to permit power transmission lines
Despite debate over its environmental impact, there is broad
agreement that the BAAJA would meaningfully improve the
permitting process. The key question is whether it can become law
under the current Congress. The Senate is expected to vote on 9
November, after which the bill would still need approval from the

House and President Trump. Democrats may want to delay the bill
and seek changes once they regain control of Congress next year,
but doing so could increase the risk of a presidential veto. This
creates an incentive to reach a deal sooner rather than later.

### Page 6

Even if the BAAJA does not pass under the current Congress,
permitting reform is likely to remain a bipartisan theme. The
growing need to expand power supply and infrastructure more
quickly will keep pressure on future Congresses to streamline
permitting.
Business implications
For energy companies – both renewables and fossil fuels –
the BAAJA could signiﬁcantly shorten permitting timelines. If
enacted, it would give project developers greater certainty,

reducing the risk of litigation and permit cancellations. This
could further expand project siting options for developers.
Nevertheless, in the very near term, projects may need to
retain ﬂexibility around their permitting strategy while the
new policy framework is developed and implemented.
Expect more protectionism and reshoring
efforts against China dominance
From clean energy technologies to the critical minerals that
underpin much of the US economy, China controls a large share of

global production capacity. Consequently, reducing reliance on
China will remain a US policy priority. Such eﬀorts would increase
rather than reduce project costs in the near term. But the long-term
beneﬁts of stronger domestic manufacturing and more secure
supply chains could outweigh those costs.
Global supply chain capacity distribution
across various sectors

Source: ING Research, BloombergNEF, International Energy Agency
This creates two key risks. First, heavy reliance on China leaves US
industries vulnerable to geopolitical tensions. We have already seen
this with rare earths, where Chinese export restrictions contributed
to disruptions for US auto manufacturers. Second, China’s supply
chain dominance can put US manufacturers at a competitive
disadvantage.
Building more secure energy and natural resource supply chains

has long attracted bipartisan support in the US. The Trump
administration has supported domestic rare earth production
through funding, purchase agreements, and equity investments. In
solar, China-focused trade and tax credit restrictions are also
supporting the expansion of domestic manufacturing.
We expect this focus to continue. The executive branch could
pursue further protective measures and is unlikely to face major
opposition from Congress. Congress could also authorise additional

funding to strengthen rare earth supply chains.
Furthermore, a Democratic-controlled Congress could push to
extend clean manufacturing tax credits as part of broader
negotiations with President Trump. Given the administration’s focus
on countering China and expanding US manufacturing, an
extension could emerge as a compromise in a broader legislative
package under an optimistic scenario. All these eﬀorts would also
extend into future administrations.

Business implications
For US companies:
Clean energy companies and other industries that rely on
rare earths, including semiconductors, AI, defence, and

electronics, should have enough policy certainty to build
long-term supplier relationships outside China. In the near
term, however, diversiﬁcation could raise supply chain costs
as alternative sources may be more expensive. Solar could
face particular cost pressures, as some tariﬀs on supply
chain equipment also apply to non-Chinese suppliers.
For overseas companies:
US eﬀorts to build supply chains outside China are creating
opportunities for manufacturers elsewhere. The US is

supporting rare earth production in Australia and Brazil, while
solar and battery suppliers in countries such as South Korea
and Japan could beneﬁt from stronger demand. This could
also encourage more manufacturing investment in the US.
Meanwhile, European clean energy developers could face
greater competition for non-Chinese supplies. While the EU
has not imposed US-style tariﬀs, it is tightening restrictions
on Chinese equipment through funding, procurement,

auction, and subsidy rules. This could intensify global
competition for non-Chinese suppliers and push costs higher.

据媒体报道， OpenAI 已于周五完成约 110 亿美元的资⾦到账，此为本轮融资的⾸期部分，
对应公司估值 1.25 万亿美元。本轮融资总规模最⾼可达 200 亿美元，剩余部分预计将于未来

摘要：
OpenAI 在 AI ⾏业情绪降温的背景下完成新⼀轮融资的⾸期交割，募资规模和估值均较原计划⼤
幅缩⽔，折射出市场对 AI 基础设施投⼊的审慎态度。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠透露，OpenAI 已于周五完成约 110 亿美元的资⾦到账，此为本轮融资的⾸
期部分，对应公司估值 1.25 万亿美元。
本轮融资总规模最⾼可达 200 亿美元，剩余部分预计将于未来数周内完成。这⼀数字较 OpenAI

最初拟定的 300 亿美元⽬标及 1.4 万亿美元估值均有明显下调。
与此同时，竞争对⼿ Anthropic 的潜在上市估值区间也从此前的 1.5 万亿美元下修⾄ 1.2 万亿⾄ 1.3
万亿美元，折射出整个 AI 板块的估值压⼒。
参与⾸批融资的投资者包括 Coatue Management 和 T. Rowe Price 。其他参与本轮融资的机构还
包括软银集团、红杉资本、 Founders Fund 和 Andreessen Horowitz 和来⾃阿联酋的投资者财
团。
此外， OpenAI 还在与投资银⾏合作，向⾼净值个⼈及零售投资者募资。
估值缩⽔背后： AI 情绪⽣变
此次融资规模的缩减，标志着这家引领当前 AI 浪潮的明星公司在融资策略上的显著转向。
OpenAI 此前曾推迟 IPO 计划，转⽽寻求⼤规模私募融资，但市场情绪已悄然改变。
市场转变的核⼼在于两重担忧：
其⼀，外界对 AI 基础设施的巨额资本⽀出可能拖累回报的顾虑持续升温；
其⼆，美国中期选举结果可能为数据中⼼建设带来新的政策阻⼒。

值得注意的是， OpenAI 上⼀轮融资于今年 3 ⽉完成，募资规模为 1220 亿美元，彼时估值为 8520
亿美元。此次 1.25 万亿美元的估值虽较三⽉仍有提升，却远低于原定⽬标，这⼀落差清晰勾勒出
市场预期在短短数⽉内的转变轨迹。

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers 
Consensus S&P500 index Q3 earnings estimates by sector   Chance of Democrats winning control of US House and Senate*

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *Polymarket probability

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary:  We retain our near -term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid -term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3 -7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15 -year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US?  Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities  among all sc enarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer -term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fa llible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bond s (CoCos1) 
and US 10 -year go vernment bond ideas:  We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10 -year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:   Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share -price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monet isation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence.  We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight , while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral  and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent  technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long -term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro  area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro  area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While Fr ance’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro  
area bank equities  offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro  area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5 -3.0% q/q , led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased , despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs . Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro  area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited , supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in  China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as  a Core holding , as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in  AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weak ened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sect or contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation , leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk -reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10 -year yield fell  over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though.  Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y . Fed officials  have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

Significant share issuance has kept the market

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds , especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell -off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals . The sustained widening of the OAT -Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near -term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating rev iews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the  French presidential election . Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near -term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe -haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The Fr ance-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to  constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves  the CHF at a sizeable interest -
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90 , excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention . A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near -term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next  12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in Fr ench government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high  public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustment s are  weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s  10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premi a on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, e levated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi -year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid -2027, reducing USD support and driving  the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk  is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding  conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our 
December quarter CPI to 1.3%qtr/3.9%yr 
and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

### Page 2

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the 
quarterly estimate. Outside of a 0.09ppt contribution to the 
revision from holiday travel & accommodation (revised up from 
4.2% to 5.6%) all else was via small contributions for a large 
number of expenditure categories. This is why there is a 0.2ppt 
increase in our September quarter TM estimate from 0.7%qtr 
to 0.9%qtr.
A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

and thus a more significant moderation in price setting behav -
iour. It is also possible that our auto fuel prices fall by more 
than we anticipate. For while we have spot crude prices to fall 
to about pre-crisis levels by end 2027, a combination of a slow 
recovery in refining capacity, constrained return of physical 
supplies and higher shipping cost keep Australian fuel prices 
above their pre-conflict level. Should all these factors improve 
fast than we expect, and auto fuel prices return to pre-conflict 
level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

## Document 418: Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V . Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”
